### Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

**Governing question:** Which employer beliefs about employee speech protect a disciplinary decision, and which do not.

**Current rule:** An employer acting on a genuine, reasonable account of speech that would be disciplinable under the governing public-employee-speech rules is protected even if that factual account is mistaken. Conversely, an unreasonable and incorrect account does not defeat a claim when the employee actually spoke protected speech and that speech caused the adverse action. The employee must show protected speech was a substantial or motivating factor; the employer may establish by a preponderance that it would have taken the same action absent the protected speech. These limited propositions do not impose liability for every investigation defect or adopt the plurality’s complete investigative procedure.

**Authority by component:**

- Seven-Justice genuine-and-reasonable-account safe harbor: [Waters v. Churchill — A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Waters_v_Churchill_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's plurality opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg, adopts this sufficient condition; Scalia's concurrence in the judgment, joined by Kennedy and Thomas, expressly accepts it under their broader genuine-ground rule. Seven Justices agree at this precise level; only four adopt the full objective-inquiry framework.
- Six-Justice unreasonable-and-false-account limit: [Waters v. Churchill — An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Waters_v_Churchill_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor, Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg accept this proposition under objective factual care; Stevens's dissent, joined by Blackmun, expressly accepts it under their broader actual-speech rule. Six Justices agree on this proposition, but the two dissenters do not join the reasonable-error safe harbor or the judgment vacating the appellate rule.
- Motive and same-decision proof: [Waters v. Churchill — The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Waters_v_Churchill_merits_1994-05-31.md#public-projection), 1994-05-31. O'Connor's four-Justice plurality and Scalia's three-Justice concurrence in the judgment support this common vacatur and limited remand. Blackmun and Stevens would affirm the appellate reversal under their different actual-speech rule; their votes are not counted as support for vacatur.

**Present operation effective:** Waters v. Churchill, 1994-05-31.

**Open question:** Unreasonable belief where the actual speech was unprotected, and a universal investigation checklist, are not resolved. The four-Justice complete reasonableness framework is noncontrolling.

**Holdings navigation:** [A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-genuine-reasonable-account-of-disciplinable-speech-supplies-a-limited-safe-harbor); [An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-unreasonable-false-account-cannot-defeat-actually-protected-speech); [The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-motive-dispute-requires-remand-without-restoring-defendants-summary-judgment).

### The vessel’s cargo-turnover warning duty

**Governing question:** Which cargo hazards a vessel must warn a competent stevedore about at turnover.

**Current rule:** The vessel must warn of cargo hazards known to it, or that it should know through the care assigned to it, likely to be encountered in unloading and neither known to nor obvious to or reasonably anticipated by a competent stevedore. The vessel has no general duty to supervise loading or inspect completed stowage for concealed defects absent a contract, law or custom imposing that duty. Knowledge and obviousness must be assessed for the correct actor at the relevant turnover time; a supplier’s knowledge is not automatically the vessel’s knowledge.

**Authority by component:**

- Assigned care, competent stevedore and actor-specific inquiry: [Howlett v. Birkdale Shipping Co., S.A — The cargo turnover-warning duty is limited by the vessel’s assigned care; Knowledge and obviousness require the correct actor and time on remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Howlett_v_Birkdale_Shipping_Co_SA_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Kennedy's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt the limited turnover-warning rule and rejection of a general cargo-inspection duty. Kennedy's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt this application and bounded vacatur and remand.

**Present operation effective:** Howlett v. Birkdale Shipping Co., S.A, 1994-06-13.

**Holdings navigation:** [The cargo turnover-warning duty is limited by the vessel’s assigned care](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-cargo-turnover-warning-duty-is-limited-by-the-vessels-assigned-care); [Knowledge and obviousness require the correct actor and time on remand](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#knowledge-and-obviousness-require-the-correct-actor-and-time-on-remand).

### Equal state enforcement for employees who collectively bargain

**Governing question:** Whether a State may withhold ordinary labor-law enforcement solely because employees completed protected collective bargaining.

**Current rule:** A State may not deny enforcement of an otherwise independent wage right solely because the employee’s collective-bargaining agreement contains an arbitration clause. Conditioning state assistance on avoiding completed collective bargaining impermissibly penalizes the federally protected activity. Section 1983 supplies a remedy for the identified individual federal labor right infringed by that policy; a generalized assertion of federal preemption alone is not thereby made an enforceable individual right.

**Authority by component:**

- Protected bargaining and the specific §1983 channel: [Livadas v. Bradshaw — California may not penalize completion of protected collective bargaining; Section 1983 supplies a channel for this identified federal labor right](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Livadas_v_Bradshaw_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Stevens's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt this statutory labor-right holding. Stevens's unanimous Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg adopt the right, nonforeclosure and bounded-remedy analysis.

**Present operation effective:** Livadas v. Bradshaw, 1994-06-13.

**Holdings navigation:** [Section 1983 supplies a channel for this identified federal labor right](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#section-1983-supplies-a-channel-for-this-identified-federal-labor-right); [California may not penalize completion of protected collective bargaining](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#california-may-not-penalize-completion-of-protected-collective-bargaining).

### FELA negligent emotional injury within the zone of danger

**Governing question:** When an employee may recover under FELA for negligently caused emotional injury.

**Current rule:** A negligently caused emotional injury can be actionable on either of two alternative routes: the employee sustained actual physical impact from the employer's negligence, or the employee was placed by that negligence in immediate risk of physical harm and the emotional injury arises from fear of harm to the employee's own safety — that fear-based qualification attaches only to the threatened-impact route, not to the physical-impact route. Ordinary negligence, genuine injury and legally sufficient causation remain necessary. Bystander distress outside that zone and ordinary work-stress claims do not qualify. The rule neither guarantees recovery from exposure alone nor requires a judicially invented numerical proximity threshold.

**Authority by component:**

- Zone-of-danger gate and surviving negligence elements: [Consolidated Rail Corp. v. Gottshall — Negligent emotional injury is actionable within the employee's zone of danger](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Consolidated_Rail_Corp_v_Gottshall_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice Thomas's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter. Five of nine participating Justices directly adopt the same operative rule; no combination of separate rationales supplies its force.
- Exclusion of the ordinary work-stress category: [Consolidated Rail Corp. v. Gottshall — Carlisle's work-stress claim is excluded and requires judgment for Conrail](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Consolidated_Rail_Corp_v_Gottshall_merits_1994-06-24.md#public-projection), 1994-06-24. Justice Thomas's Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter. Five Justices adopt both the legal exclusion and mandatory defendant-judgment remedy.

**Present operation effective:** Consolidated Rail Corp. v. Gottshall, 1994-06-24.

**Open question:** The Court does not decide whether every qualifying claim additionally requires physical manifestations of the emotional injury.

**Holdings navigation:** [Negligent emotional injury is actionable within the employee's zone of danger](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#negligent-emotional-injury-is-actionable-within-the-employees-zone-of-danger); [Carlisle's work-stress claim is excluded and requires judgment for Conrail](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#carlisles-work-stress-claim-is-excluded-and-requires-judgment-for-conrail).

## Standards and Tests selection: Reproductive liberty and abortion regulation

## Reproductive liberty and abortion regulation

### Protected choice and the six-step standard for previability regulation

**Governing question:** Whether a legal obligation regulating previability abortion impermissibly interferes with the patient's protected decision or access to timely, clinically appropriate care.

**Current rule:** Before viability the patient decides whether to continue pregnancy without proving medical necessity or obtaining official approval of her reasons. The State may not prohibit that choice, confer effective control on another person, or prevent timely necessary life-and-health care. After viability, prohibition requires the life-and-health safeguard, including individualized physical and mental health considerations. Viability is individualized and medically supported, not a fixed constitutional week. Clinical appropriateness concerns safe and suitable care, not official approval of the patient's reasons; not every preference is a medical emergency. The following complete standard governs throughout previability:

1. **Identify the duty and protected baseline.** Identify the enacted command, authoritative construction, exceptions, enforcement consequences and relevant medical circumstances. Distinguish an actual obligation from assumed future administration. A previability prohibition, effective third-party veto or denial of timely necessary life-and-health care violates the baseline and cannot be rescued by justification. Necessary care must remain available before imminent death or catastrophic deterioration. Defining all noncompliant treatment as unsafe does not establish medical necessity.
2. **Identify actual function and purpose.** Identify what the obligation does and the asserted permissible objective, including safety, informed consent, truthful information, permissible parental involvement, health administration or financial accountability. The State may prefer childbirth and communicate truthful information. Moral disapproval does not independently justify obstruction, punishment of protected choice or burdens imposed for their deterrent effect. An obstructive purpose established by objective evidence of text, design, context and operation independently invalidates the requirement; a legislator's opposition to abortion alone does not establish that purpose. Voluntary persuasion differs from prevention through barriers. Evaluate each justification on its terms: medical claims need medical support, and deliberative claims need an explanation linking the particular obligation to informed decisionmaking.
3. **Establish actual interference, comparison and affected population.** Challengers must establish concrete additional interference caused by the challenged obligation. Relevant effects include extra appointments, material expense, consequential delay, loss of usable provider capacity, clinical risk, coercion and disclosure consequences affecting choice or access. Compare the actual system with and without the obligation while holding other relevant law and circumstances constant. For a challenged interacting package, identify the package and compare the system without it. Consider cumulative effects without counting the same burden twice. Define the affected population from the duty's operation before deciding constitutional injury, include evidence-supported subgroups, and keep the population stable. Do not average the burden across everyone pregnant or define the group as only those who prevail. Poverty, distance, transportation, employment and abuse matter insofar as they explain the State's added condition; identify that causal link. Exceptions, bypasses and alternative providers count only if actually usable. Meaningful interference means concrete, nontrivial additional burden on obtaining care or exercising the protected decision. It triggers review rather than establishing invalidity. No universal dollar, mileage or hourly threshold applies. Reliable clinical evidence, records, testimony and reasonable predictions can establish sufficiently demonstrated prospective injury before access is lost.
4. **Apply the substantive protections in order.** First, a baseline violation, unconstitutional obstructive purpose or substantial obstacle is independently invalid. A substantial obstacle seriously obstructs the affected population's practical opportunity for timely, clinically appropriate protected care; absolute impossibility is unnecessary, and success by some patients does not establish adequate access for others. A compelling interest cannot rescue an established obstacle. Second, remaining severe interference requires a compelling permissible justification and narrow tailoring. Identify the severe intrusion and explain why the independent prohibitions did not already decide the issue; overlapping protections require no artificial boundary, but an obstacle may not be relabeled as justifiable severe interference. Serious intrusion into clinical judgment, health or protected confidentiality may require this scrutiny despite usable access. Third, for remaining meaningful interference the State must substantiate a permissible justification and its fit to the added obligation. Abstract interests and conclusory assertions are insufficient. When challengers identify a concrete, practicable, substantially less burdensome alternative adequately serving the objective, the State must answer it; inadequacy, unavailability or material countervailing consequences may answer that showing. The State need not select the cheapest imaginable policy or disprove every hypothetical alternative. Reliable experience and reasonable predictions may suffice; particular legislative findings, a new study in every case, scientific certainty and numerical benefit calculations are not required. Once justification and fit are established, courts may not substitute their preferred policy. Below meaningful interference this heightened access inquiry ends, while prohibited-purpose claims, independent constitutional protections and ordinary lawful-regulation requirements remain available.
5. **Protect clinical judgment and truthful communication.** Evaluate medical justifications against competent evidence, accepted professional standards, the patient's circumstances and substantial contrary evidence. Substantial professional agreement receives serious evidentiary weight, without a professional association's veto or conclusive weight for a clinician's bare assertion or an official's unsupported preference. Necessary care must remain timely; unnecessary committee vetoes and documentation functioning as prior permission may not displace appropriate clinical judgment. Duties must be sufficiently definite and applicable fault requirements respected. Later reasonable medical disagreement alone does not establish every element of a punishable offense; lawful enforcement against fraud, malpractice and professional misconduct remains. Required medical information must be accurate, nonmisleading and material to informed consent. Concise factual assistance notices may inform choice without ideological assent; clinicians may explain limitations and correct misinformation. Accuracy does not alone validate burdensome timing, delivery or certification. Distinguish useful confidential oversight records from public disclosure and records enforcing invalid obligations.
6. **Identify the violation and tailor relief.** Specify the duty or application that fails, why, and the corresponding relief. Preserve independently lawful requirements under applicable severability law and expressly address dependent certifications, report fields and sanctions. An individual claim does not fail because others comply; broader relief requires a corresponding showing. An indivisible mandatory condition may be invalidated when its ordinary operation imposes demonstrated meaningful interference and the State fails to justify it. Voluntary compliance in some circumstances does not alone validate compulsory compliance. Otherwise confine relief to the established applications or groups. This qualification of a strict no-valid-applications approach does not remove proof of causation, scope or inadequate justification. For cumulative burdens, remedy the challenged obligation's or package's contribution without automatically invalidating all regulation. Replacing the trimester timetable does not dissolve injunctions or authorize enforcement of every challenged provision; the mandate must specify the resulting duties.

**Authority by component:**

- Protected previability choice, individualized viability and necessary life-and-health protection: [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, “The framework retaining protected choice”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; Stone's opinion joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter.
- All six steps, including the independent obstacle bar, residual severe-interference scrutiny, meaningful lesser-burden substantiation, proof allocation, clinical safeguards and relief: *Casey*, “The standard governing previability regulation after this decision”; Stone's opinion joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Souter. These five direct joins supply the whole rule; no *Marks* synthesis is needed.
- Integrated displacement of the independent trimester timetable: *Casey*, “The trimester timetable and the timing of regulation”; the same five-Justice rationale. The other four votes against continuing the timetable do not join the replacement framework.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Burdens and proof:** Challengers establish the duty, population, causation and triggering interference. The State then bears the applicable justification and tailoring or fit burden. This does not alter ordinary proof of disputed historical facts or the enacted burden for a separate statutory defense. No State substantiation burden arises merely because a law concerns abortion.

**Exceptions and limits:** Gestational age may support a particular medically justified safety requirement; crossing a trimester boundary does not itself change the standard. The rule creates no general professional immunity, funding entitlement, constitutional fetal-personhood rule, conscience ruling, general right to a particular drug, or universal numerical facial-review formula. It does not decide every postviability restriction.

**Material development:** The independent trimester timing restrictions are displaced only with adoption of this complete substitute. Protected choice, viability and necessary life-and-health care survive. Absence of a substantial obstacle no longer ends the inquiry into demonstrated meaningful interference.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Scope of prospective relief for abortion requirements](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Reproductive liberty and constitutional precedent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-5).

### Spousal notification that gives practical control over protected choice

**Governing question:** Whether compulsory spousal notice and certification may condition an adult patient's protected previability decision.

**Current rule:** A notification requirement is invalid when its proved operation gives spouses practical control over protected decisions for a large fraction of the independently defined women compelled to disclose against their wishes, after accounting for usable exceptions. Voluntary consultation by other patients does not cure that restriction. Identify the compelled group before deciding injury; do not count willing disclosure as a constitutionally valid restrictive application or impose a universal numerical threshold. Formal notice rather than consent does not answer practical prevention. Severance and relief against dependent certification, enforcement and notice-compliance reporting must address the unconstitutional duty; a separately lawful confidential demographic field does not fall merely because it shares a subsection.

**Authority by component:**

- Effective control, affected-group review and notice-specific facial qualification: [Casey, “Spousal notice and effective control over the protected decision”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter directly support the rationale.
- Dependent reporting versus independently severable demographic information: *Casey*, “Notice-compliance and nonnotification-reason reports” (the same six) and “The independently severable marital-status field” (all nine).

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The invalidated §3209 allowed substitute signed statements that the spouse was not the father, could not be located after diligent effort, that pregnancy resulted from spousal sexual assault under §3128 reported to an agency with jurisdiction, or that the woman had reason to believe notification was likely to cause bodily injury to her by her spouse or another person; medical emergency separately excused compliance. The incorporated §3128(c) condition required personal reporting by the victim or her agent within 90 days of the assault. Statements required no notarization but had to warn against punishable false statements. These exceptions did not generally reach psychological coercion, financial retaliation, harm to children or others, exposure of private information, or divorce and custody threats. The holding does not independently adjudicate §3128(c)'s criminal-law reporting requirement. That reporting condition does not limit the distinct rape-omission permission for paternal-support information.

**Related current rules:** “Protected choice and the six-step standard for previability regulation” supplies the five-Justice independently dispositive obstacle rule. Kennedy joins this notice-specific rationale without joining that whole standard.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion reporting and public disclosure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-3); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Spousal control of reproductive choice](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-6).

### Medical emergency and the separate disclosure-health defense

**Governing question:** How Pennsylvania's emergency, emergency-explanation and harmful-information provisions operate independently.

**Current rule:** Section 3203 defines emergency through a physician's good-faith clinical judgment that immediate abortion is needed to avert death or that delay creates serious risk of substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function. It is sustained under the prospective construction that compliance cannot create a significant threat to life or health, permitting treatment of preeclampsia, inevitable abortion and prematurely ruptured membranes at onset rather than after catastrophic deterioration. Under §3205(b), the physician must explain the medical indications before emergency abortion only if possible; explanation is not advance permission or a reason to delay necessary care. Separately, §3205(c) protects a physician from liability for omitting §3205(a) information upon proof by a preponderance of a reasonable belief that furnishing it would severely adversely affect the patient's physical or mental health. Actual occurrence of the feared injury is unnecessary. That defense neither supplies a waiting-period waiver nor replaces emergency treatment protection.

**Authority by component:**

- Emergency construction: [Casey, “Medical emergency and the protective construction of §3203”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992.
- Conditional prior explanation: *Casey*, “Emergency explanation under §3205(b).”
- Separate defense and enacted proof burden: *Casey*, “The distinct information-omission defense in §3205(c).” All three are unanimous direct holdings.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The significant-threat interpretation is the Court's adoption of the Third Circuit's prospective construction, not extra statutory language or a binding Pennsylvania Supreme Court construction. No separate emergency-vagueness holding or decision about every clinical risk or postviability restriction follows. The statutory defense's burden remains distinct from the constitutional proof allocation.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion emergency care](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-1); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion informed consent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-2).

### Accurate abortion-consent information, optional materials and receipt certification

**Governing question:** What Pennsylvania may require patients to be told, offered or asked to certify in obtaining informed consent, including independent First Amendment limits.

**Current rule:** Section 3205(a)(1) may require accurate, medically material information about the procedure, material risks and alternatives, probable gestational age and childbirth risks. The three §3205(a)(2) notices may state truthfully: state materials concerning fetal development and alternatives are available and a free copy will be supplied if chosen; medical assistance for prenatal care, childbirth and neonatal care may be available; and the father is liable to assist in support even if he offered to pay for abortion. Assistance and legal liability are not promises of eligibility, payment or collection. Paternal-support information **may** be omitted in a rape pregnancy, including spousal rape, without a police-report condition; omission is permitted, not required, and changes no support obligation. Section 3208 publication and §3205(a)(3) chosen receipt are permissible at their objective, accurate, nonjudgmental scope. Notice of availability is distinct from receipt: patients need not accept, read or endorse materials or listed agencies. Clinicians may correct or criticize them. Section 3205(a)(4) certification may record actual receipt of lawful information, not ideological agreement, unchosen reading or compliance with an invalid prerequisite. The §3205(c) disclosure-health defense remains available on its own terms.

The First Amendment independently permits these bounded medical and ancillary disclosures, voluntary receipt and factual documentation. Payment does not strip professional speech of protection, and truth alone does not justify every compulsory statement. False, irrelevant, coercive or ideological scripts and materially different implementation remain outside the sustained duties. Accurate content does not establish the validity of a timing or exclusive-speaker condition; invalid timing and delivery obligations cannot survive through a certificate.

**Authority by component:**

- Medical content, objective publication/chosen receipt and basic certification: [Casey, “Accurate material medical information,” “Statutory publication and chosen receipt of materials,” and “Certification of lawful receipt”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; unanimous direct holdings at those scopes.
- Each ancillary notice and its qualification: *Casey*, “Notice of available free materials,” “Notice of potentially available medical assistance,” and “Paternal-support notice and the rape omission permission”; Stone, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter supply five rationale joins. The additional judgment votes do not broaden that rationale.
- Independent speech rules: *Casey*, the three “Independent speech challenge” holdings; Souter with White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas for medical content, chosen materials and basic certification; the same group without Blackmun for ancillary notices. Stone's judgment-only agreements supply no speech rationale.
- Dependent timing and delivery links: *Casey*, “Certification of lawful receipt,” with the five-Justice invalidation grounds in “Physician delivery of medical information” and “The mandatory 24-hour interval.”

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The rape omission permission has no §3209/§3128 reporting or 90-day condition. No finding establishes universal accuracy of actual publications or agencies. No general commercial-speech classification or professional-speech immunity is adopted. Required content remains subject to the separate access framework.

**Material development:** Akron and Thornburgh are qualified only insofar as they categorically prohibit the sustained concise, accurate ancillary notices because they may encourage childbirth; their protection against misleading, coercive and unjustifiably burdensome implementation remains.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion informed consent](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-2); [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion information and compelled speech](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-7).

### One-parent informed consent with an independent judicial bypass

**Governing question:** The permissible operation of Pennsylvania §3206's parental-consent structure for unemancipated minors under eighteen.

**Current rule:** The minor's informed consent and one parent's informed consent ordinarily suffice, with emergency treatment excused from compliance. Only the mother's consent is required when the father participated in incest. A guardian may consent when both parents are deceased or unavailable within reasonable time and manner; after divorce the custodial parent's consent suffices; if neither parent nor guardian is so available, an adult standing in place of a parent may consent. The parent or guardian considers only the minor's best interests. The minor may elect court authorization without first seeking parental consent. Maturity and capacity with actual informed consent, or alternatively best interests, require authorization through a confidential, expedited process. Counsel is provided absent private counsel or knowing, intelligent waiver; the trial ruling is due within three business days of application and appeal ruling within five business days of filing. Proceedings and records are confidential. Necessary emergency care cannot await those proceedings.

Parental informed consent may use the accurate medical content and qualified materials, assistance and paternal-support notices described in “Accurate abortion-consent information, optional materials and receipt certification,” including optional materials and permission to omit paternal-support information after rape without a police report. This construction creates no parent-specific waiting period, physician-only delivery requirement or compulsory clinic visit. A bypass is not a universal answer to the burdens of a separate obligation.

**Authority by component:**

- Structure, statutory substitutions, emergency exception and alternate bypass routes: [Casey, “One-parent informed consent and independent judicial bypass”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; unanimous rationale.
- Parental information and nonincorporation of additional timing, speaker or attendance obligations: *Casey*, “Accurate information used for informed parental consent”; Souter, White, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas supply seven rationale joins. Stone agrees only in the judgment on this component.
- No bypass-based approval of an unestablished extra visit: *Casey*, “The parental-attendance objection and the denial of special relief”; Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Souter supply the controlling ground.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** These are the sustained statutory safeguards, not a holding that every one is constitutionally indispensable everywhere. The lower court's use of §3205 to explain parental information is a construction, not enacted incorporation of all adult mechanisms. No independent adult-incapacity claim or hypothetical compulsory-visit operation is decided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Minor consent and independent authorization](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-4).

### Confidential abortion reporting and distinct medical predicates

**Governing question:** The lawful scope of Pennsylvania's confidential medical-basis reporting duties and severable demographic collection.

**Current rule:** Section 3214(a)(8) may require the basis for a physician's judgment that abortion under §3211(b)(1)'s rule for twenty-four or more weeks was necessary to prevent death or substantial and irreversible impairment of a major bodily function. The report does not create a new substantive necessity test or decide every other later-abortion restriction. Section 3214(a)(10) may require an after-the-fact account of the judgment that a §3203 emergency excused compliance; it creates no advance-permission or pretreatment-report condition and does not narrow the prospective emergency construction. Section 3214(a)(11), incorporating §3210(a), may require the actual inquiries, examinations, tests and diagnostic basis used to determine probable gestational age under the prudent-physician standard. It does not command additional medically unnecessary tests, and §3210(a)'s exception when medical emergency prevents compliance remains. Individual reports omit patient names and retain the confidentiality safeguards accepted in the decision: the reports are not public records; law-enforcement access requires a common pleas court order after an application showing good cause; and the Department's statistical publication must not disclose any physician's, facility's, or patient's identity. Recording clinical judgment neither establishes a new offense from later professional disagreement alone nor provides general immunity from lawful enforcement. Separately, §3214(a)(12)'s confidential marital-status field may survive severance of the invalid spousal-notice and nonnotification-reason fields, without reconstructing those duties.

**Authority by component:**

- Each distinct medical field and its construction: [Casey, “Confidential reporting of medical necessity under §3214(a)(8),” “Confidential reporting of an emergency judgment under §3214(a)(10),” and “Confidential reporting of the diagnostic basis under §3214(a)(11)”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Planned_Parenthood_of_Southeastern_Pennsylvania_v_Casey_merits_1992-06-29.md), June 29, 1992; unanimous direct holdings.
- Independent demographic field: *Casey*, “The independently severable marital-status field”; unanimous direct holding.

**Present operation effective:** *Casey*, June 29, 1992.

**Exceptions and limits:** The separate gestational-age result field and unappealed underlying ascertainment/collection duties are not newly adjudicated. Confidential collection does not itself justify public release, and confidentiality alone does not answer a demonstrated access burden. The public-release relief concerning §§3207(b) and 3214(f) is confined to the plaintiff facilities' covered reports; extension requires its own evidentiary and procedural basis. No general immunity from financial accountability or governmental entitlement to intimate information follows.

**Holdings navigation:** [Planned Parenthood of Southeastern Pennsylvania v. Casey, 505 U.S. 833 (1992) — Abortion reporting and public disclosure](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#planned-parenthood-of-southeastern-pennsylvania-v-casey-505-us-833-1992-3).

## Standards and Tests selection: Speech, association, and elections

## Speech, association, and elections

### Protection of objectors' compulsory bar dues before ideological use

**Governing question:** What protection an integrated bar must provide after a member makes known continuing opposition to financing nongermane ideological activity.

**Current rule:** A continuing general objection suffices; the bar may not require repeated objections to each announced position or leave the reasonably disputed share available for use until a new objection arrives. It must give adequate advance explanation, protect that share before use through a reasonable advance reduction or adequate interest-bearing escrow, and provide reasonably prompt impartial review. The bar retains the burden to justify chargeability. Estimates and protection of a reasonably sufficient amount may address uncertainty about future activities; later reimbursement and interest do not cure temporary compelled ideological financing. No universal opt-in system is required.

**Authority by component:**

- Continuing objection, advance protection, chargeability burden, and retained alternatives: [Gibson v. Florida Bar — Timing and scope of protection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Gibson_v_Florida_Bar_merits_1991-12-04.md), December 4, 1991; Stone's opinion joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, and Thomas.

**Present operation effective:** *Gibson*, December 4, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [Gibson v. Florida Bar, 502 U.S. 104 (1991) — Compulsory bar dues](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#gibson-v-florida-bar-502-us-104-1991).

### Impartiality of balanced bar-dues arbitration panels

**Governing question:** Whether compulsory bar membership alone disqualifies arbitrators reviewing dues objections.

**Current rule:** Bar membership alone does not establish constitutional bias where the procedure gives the bar and objector one selection each and requires a mutually selected chair or judicial appointment if agreement fails. Actual bias, a direct stake, obstructive delay, and ineffectiveness in operation remain reviewable. The rule rejects a facial membership-only disqualification and does not conclusively approve any particular panel's operation.

**Authority by component:**

- Balanced selection and membership-only objection; retained operational challenges: [Gibson v. Florida Bar — Impartial review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Gibson_v_Florida_Bar_merits_1991-12-04.md), December 4, 1991; Stone's opinion joined on this proposition by all nine Justices.

**Present operation effective:** *Gibson*, December 4, 1991.

**Holdings navigation:** [Gibson v. Florida Bar, 502 U.S. 104 (1991) — Compulsory bar dues](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#gibson-v-florida-bar-502-us-104-1991).

### Authorized party expansion and independently qualified slate components

**Governing question:** Whether a State may deny ballot access through a geographic party-name barrier or failure of candidates in a different electorate to qualify.

**Current rule:** A State may require meaningful support in each separate electorate. It may not categorically bar authorized candidates from expanding an existing party under its name, or disqualify otherwise qualified countywide and city candidates solely because candidates in a different district lack sufficient signatures. Protection against unauthorized appropriation of a party's name does not justify a geographic barrier that also prohibits authorized expansion; support requirements for one electorate do not justify invalidating candidates who qualified in their own constituencies.

**Authority by component:**

- Authorized name expansion, independent qualification, and separate-electorate support: [Norman v. Reed — Ballot-access holding and operative rule](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Norman_v_Reed_merits_1992-01-14.md), January 14, 1992; Souter's opinion joined by Stone, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, and Kennedy.

**Present operation effective:** *Norman*, January 14, 1992.

**Open question:** Other district structures, cumulative burdens, and actual unauthorized name use remain outside this holding.

**Holdings navigation:** [Norman v. Reed, 502 U.S. 279 (1992) — Ballot access and political parties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#norman-v-reed-502-us-279-1992-1).

### Voting Rights Act §5 coverage of reallocations of governing authority

**Governing question:** Whether a transfer of duties among government officials changes a voting practice subject to preclearance.

**Current rule:** Section 5 requires a direct relation to voting; a redistribution of governing authority does not qualify merely because it diminishes an elected official's influence over a function. Changes in voting procedure, candidacy, electorate, or method of office selection supply familiar but nonexhaustive covered categories. Reallocation of road funds within an elected commission and transfer of road operations to an appointed engineer while the elected commission retains substantial appointment and budget authority do not, on those premises, require preclearance. The Attorney General's interpretation receives substantial consideration within the statutory boundary, but a significant-transfer/different-constituencies formulation cannot substitute for a connection to voting. Postelection timing alone creates no exemption, and coverage remains separate from discriminatory purpose or effect.

**Authority by component:**

- Voting/governing boundary and retained-office scope: [Presley v. Etowah County Commission — The two resolutions do not change a voting practice](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Presley_v_Etowah_County_Commission_merits_1992-01-27.md), January 27, 1992.
- Limit on administrative and different-constituencies constructions: [Presley — Administrative interpretation does not eliminate the voting boundary](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Presley_v_Etowah_County_Commission_merits_1992-01-27.md), January 27, 1992. Kennedy's opinion joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, and Thomas controls both.

**Present operation effective:** *Presley*, January 27, 1992.

**Open question:** Whether an extreme transfer effectively replacing an elective office with an appointive office requires preclearance is unresolved. The decision does not dispose of independent §2, Title VI, or constitutional claims.

**Holdings navigation:** [Presley v. Etowah County Commission; Mack v. Russell County Commission, 502 U.S. 491 (1992) — Voting Rights Act preclearance](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#presley-v-etowah-county-commission-mack-v-russell-county-commission-502-us-491-1992).

### Write-in voting within the complete ballot-access system

**Governing question:** Whether a ban on write-in voting violates electoral-choice or independent expressive rights.

**Current rule:** Under the existing Anderson inquiry, the court assesses the character and magnitude of the actual injury against the State's precise interests and the extent to which those interests require the burden. Severe burdens require narrow tailoring to a compelling interest; reasonable, nondiscriminatory burdens ordinarily may be justified by important regulatory interests. When the complete access system imposes only reasonable burdens, a write-in prohibition is presumptively valid and may be sustained by proportionate interests, including primary finality and prevention of evasive candidacies or party raiding. The ballot need not provide an official count and publication of votes for fictional figures or arbitrary protest messages as an independent expressive entitlement.

**Authority by component:**
- Governing inherited framework: [Burdick v. Takushi, “1. Review of election regulations follows the actual burden”](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1991/records/Burdick_v_Takushi_merits_1992-06-08.md), June 8, 1992; White, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Thomas (five), applying Anderson and the earlier simulated Norman rule.
- Limited-burden write-in presumption: Burdick, “2. Hawaii's complete write-in prohibition is valid in its reasonably accessible system,” June 8, 1992; same five Justices.
- Arbitrary-message claim: Burdick, “3. The ballot need not serve as an official tally of arbitrary political messages,” June 8, 1992; same controlling five-Justice opinion, with all nine agreeing in rejection of that component.

**Present operation effective:** Burdick v. Takushi, June 8, 1992, for the write-in presumption and expressive boundary; the underlying Anderson framework is applied unchanged.

**Exceptions and limits:** No absolute validation of all write-in bans follows. Materially more restrictive access systems may impose severe burdens. The rule does not replace strict scrutiny of content-based campaign restrictions on public sidewalks.

**Holdings navigation:** [Burdick v. Takushi, 504 U.S. 428 (1992) — Ballot access and political parties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#burdick-v-takushi-504-us-428-1992).

### Commercial-speech advancement and fit in selective restrictions

**Governing question:** What instructions govern commercial-speech advancement and fit in selective restrictions?

**Current rule:** For truthful, nonmisleading commercial speech about lawful activity, the government must establish real harms, material advancement and a reasonable fit between its chosen restriction and its interests. Where commercial and newspaper racks cause the same physical harms, the commercial category requires justification tied to those harms; benign objectives alone do not make content-selected regulation content neutral. Incremental regulation is not invalid merely because its reduction is small, but alternatives bear on fit without a least-restrictive-means or all-sources-at-once requirement. In professional solicitation the government must demonstrate advancement in the relevant professional setting, without an invariable empirical-study requirement. Deceptive or coercive solicitation remains regulable. Central Hudson and Fox remain the framework; no general equality of commercial and noncommercial speech or categorical vice exception follows.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. The selected commercial category lacks the required fit: [Cincinnati v. Discovery Network — 1. The selected commercial category lacks the required fit](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Cincinnati_v_Discovery_Network_merits_1993-03-24.md), 1993-03-24. Stevens's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter: seven, throughout.
- 2. The selective prohibition is not content neutral: [Cincinnati v. Discovery Network — 2. The selective prohibition is not content neutral](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Cincinnati_v_Discovery_Network_merits_1993-03-24.md), 1993-03-24. Stevens's Court opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Souter: seven. This rejects the City's independent time, place and manner defense.
- Real advancement must be established in the relevant professional setting: [Edenfield v. Fane — Real advancement must be established in the relevant professional setting](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/edenfield_merits_1993-04-26.md), 1993-04-26. Kennedy’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority agreement at this level of generality; no Marks inference.

**Present operation effective:** Cincinnati v. Discovery Network, March 24, 1993, for the selective-newsrack fit and content distinction. Central Hudson and Fox supply the retained advancement requirement applied in Edenfield, April 26, 1993; that application creates no separate test.

**Holdings navigation:** [Cincinnati v. Discovery Network, 507 U.S. 410 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#cincinnati-v-discovery-network-507-us-410-1993); [Edenfield v. Fane, 507 U.S. 761 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#edenfield-v-fane-507-us-761-1993).

### Crime-related motive evidence and intentional racial victim selection

**Governing question:** What instructions govern crime-related motive evidence and intentional racial victim selection?

**Current rule:** Authoritative state construction fixes statutory meaning, but the federal court independently determines constitutional effect. The First Amendment permits an enhanced penalty for an independently criminal battery when the defendant intentionally selects the victim because of race. Lawful evidence may prove relevant motive; abstract racist belief, expression or association cannot be punished without connection to the offense or another legitimate issue. Speculative suppression of views from fear of their later evidentiary use in a future crime does not establish substantial overbreadth. Evidentiary safeguards and Dawson's relevance limit remain; no independent speech offense or blanket admissibility rule follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Federal constitutional effect is not settled by a state court’s characterization: [Wisconsin v. Mitchell — Federal constitutional effect is not settled by a state court’s characterization](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/mitchell_merits_1993-06-11.md), 1993-06-11. White’s Parts I and II; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Intentional racial selection can aggravate an independently criminal battery: [Wisconsin v. Mitchell — Intentional racial selection can aggravate an independently criminal battery](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/mitchell_merits_1993-06-11.md), 1993-06-11. White’s Parts I and II; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Speculative future evidentiary use does not establish substantial overbreadth: [Wisconsin v. Mitchell — Speculative future evidentiary use does not establish substantial overbreadth](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/mitchell_merits_1993-06-11.md), 1993-06-11. White’s Part III; White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-11; Wisconsin v. Mitchell, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Wisconsin v. Mitchell, 508 U.S. 476 (1993)](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#wisconsin-v-mitchell-508-us-476-1993).

### Expressive-enterprise forfeiture and independent First Amendment review

**Governing question:** What instructions govern expressive-enterprise forfeiture and independent First Amendment review?

**Current rule:** Postconviction RICO forfeiture of identified assets linked to adjudicated racketeering is not a traditional prior restraint merely because it impairs future publishing where it neither prohibits publication nor requires permission. That classification and valid convictions do not dispose of an independent First Amendment challenge to forfeiture of an expressive enterprise and removal of lawful inventory. Courts must independently examine the expressive burden, distinguishing judicial forfeiture from subsequent administrative destruction and confining correction to reviewable injury and available relief. No categorical inventory immunity or comprehensive scrutiny framework is adopted. Excessive Fines review remains independent.

**Authority by component:**

- 1. This postconviction forfeiture is not a traditional prior restraint: [Alexander v. United States — 1. This postconviction forfeiture is not a traditional prior restraint](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Alexander_v_United_States_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Souter's Part I, joined by Stone-Zsela, White, O'Connor, Scalia, Thomas; six Justices adopt this proposition. Blackmun, Stevens and Kennedy do not join the classification. The independent First Amendment question has a different coalition.
- 2. The forfeiture approval requires an independent First Amendment examination: [Alexander v. United States — 2. The forfeiture approval requires an independent First Amendment examination](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1992/records/Alexander_v_United_States_merits_1993-06-28.md), 1993-06-28. Souter's Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy; five Justices expressly adopt this limited rule and remand. Their separate positions do not establish a majority rule either categorically immunizing expressive inventory or adopting a comprehensive scrutiny framework for every postconviction forfeiture.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-28; Alexander v. United States, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** [Alexander v. United States](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#alexander-v-united-states); [Alexander v. United States](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#alexander-v-united-states-1).

### Residential signs and preservation of a distinctive speech medium

**Governing question:** Whether a municipality may largely eliminate the homeowner’s own-sign channel of expression.

**Current rule:** A municipality cannot virtually eliminate residential signs when the channel has distinctive communicative value and the offered substitutes do not adequately replace it, even assuming the ordinance is content neutral. A sign at the speaker’s own home combines location, attribution, cost and accessibility in ways that handbills, newspapers or other channels may not reproduce. Legitimate aesthetic and safety interests permit appropriate physical regulation, but do not themselves justify the near-total loss of that medium.

**Authority by component:**

- Distinctive residential channel and inadequate alternatives: [City of Ladue v. Gilleo — The replacement prohibition leaves no adequate residential channel](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/City_of_Ladue_v_Gilleo_merits_1994-06-13.md#public-projection), 1994-06-13. Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine adopt the same residential-channel holding and limited remedy; no fractured-opinion inference is necessary.

**Present operation effective:** City of Ladue v. Gilleo, 1994-06-13.

**Open question:** No fixed size, number or duration limit, categorical exemption scheme or facial ruling on every content distinction is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** [The replacement prohibition leaves no adequate residential channel](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-replacement-prohibition-leaves-no-adequate-residential-channel).

### Cable-carriage rules and review of legislative predictions

**Governing question:** How the First Amendment reviews content-neutral cable-carriage requirements.

**Current rule:** Cable operators’ editorial choices receive ordinary First Amendment protection, without the broadcast-scarcity rationale. Content classification examines what the law selects and why. The general full-power must-carry provisions at issue are content neutral and receive intermediate scrutiny: they must advance substantial interests unrelated to suppressing expression and burden no substantially greater amount of speech than necessary. The least restrictive alternative is not required. Preserving broadcast availability, diverse information sources and fair competition are substantial interests, but identifying them does not prove the law satisfies the test. Legislative predictive judgments receive substantial deference while courts independently determine whether reasonable inferences rest on substantial evidence and support the claimed harms and response.

**Authority by component:**

- Protected expression and full-power content classification: [Turner Broadcasting System, Inc. v. FCC — Cable editorial expression receives ordinary First Amendment protection; Content classification depends on what the law selects and why; The general full-power carriage requirements are content neutral](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Turner_Broadcasting_System_Inc_v_FCC_merits_1994-06-27.md#public-projection), 1994-06-27. Kennedy's opinion, Parts II-A and II-B as relevant to this proposition, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: eight Justices. Stevens recognizes protected cable expression but does not join these complete portions and is not counted for their formulation. Kennedy's opinion, Part II-B, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: eight Justices. Stevens does not join that portion; his agreement with the case-specific classification below is distinct. Kennedy's opinion, Parts II-C and II-D, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter: five Justices. O'Connor, Scalia, Thomas and Ginsburg reject this application.
- Intermediate scrutiny and substantial interests: [Turner Broadcasting System, Inc. v. FCC — Intermediate scrutiny requires advancement and a proportionate speech burden; Preserving broadcast availability, diverse sources and fair competition are substantial interests](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Turner_Broadcasting_System_Inc_v_FCC_merits_1994-06-27.md#public-projection), 1994-06-27. Kennedy's opinion, Part III-A, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter: five Justices. This common standard does not establish common agreement about whether the existing record satisfies it. Kennedy's opinion, Part III-A, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter: five Justices. The separate opinions disputing the neutrality of the Act's localism and programming preferences do not join this proposition.
- Five-Justice agreement on deferential but independent evidentiary review: [Turner Broadcasting System, Inc. v. FCC — Predictive judgments receive substantial deference and remain subject to review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Turner_Broadcasting_System_Inc_v_FCC_merits_1994-06-27.md#public-projection), 1994-06-27. Kennedy's Part III-B, supported by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun and Souter, together with Stevens's express agreement in his concurrence at this limited level of generality: five Justices. Stevens does not join Part III-B, its real-harm formulation as a complete passage, or its record-insufficiency application. This is direct agreement on the stated proposition across writings, not a Marks inference or a fifth join in the plurality portion.

**Present operation effective:** Turner Broadcasting System, Inc. v. FCC, 1994-06-27.

**Open question:** The decision does not finally sustain the provisions or classify every low-power carriage rule. The four-Justice conclusion about this record’s evidentiary insufficiency is not itself a generally controlling finding.

**Holdings navigation:** [Cable editorial expression receives ordinary First Amendment protection](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#cable-editorial-expression-receives-ordinary-first-amendment-protection); [Content classification depends on what the law selects and why](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#content-classification-depends-on-what-the-law-selects-and-why); [The general full-power carriage requirements are content neutral](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-general-full-power-carriage-requirements-are-content-neutral); [Intermediate scrutiny requires advancement and a proportionate speech burden](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#intermediate-scrutiny-requires-advancement-and-a-proportionate-speech-burden); [Preserving broadcast availability, diverse sources and fair competition are substantial interests](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#preserving-broadcast-availability-diverse-sources-and-fair-competition-are-substantial-interests); [Predictive judgments receive substantial deference and remain subject to review](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#predictive-judgments-receive-substantial-deference-and-remain-subject-to-review).

### Speech burdens in a content-neutral injunction

**Governing question:** How a court tailors a content-neutral injunction regulating speech-related conduct.

**Current rule:** A content-neutral injunction must burden no more speech than necessary to serve the significant governmental interest supporting the remedy. This inquiry is more demanding than the ordinary content-neutral legislative time, place and manner rule, without becoming a universal least-restrictive-means test. Each restriction needs a remedial connection to the established interest and conduct. Public access, medical quiet and residential privacy may support relief, but neither those interests nor prior misconduct justifies territorial restrictions or advance-permission conditions broader than necessary. Removing an invalid restriction also removes its operative predicate; it cannot be restored by relabeling the same conduct under another clause.

**Authority by component:**

- Content classification and five-Justice injunction standard: [Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc — The decree as confined is not categorically content based; A neutral speech injunction must burden no more speech than necessary](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Madsen_v_Womens_Health_Center_Inc_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. O'Connor's Part II-A(2), joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg: six. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas reject this application while accepting the separate actual-concert principle. O'Connor's Part II-B, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg: five. Stevens's individualized equitable account and the strict-restraint dissent are not joins in this standard.
- Removal of invalidated predicates and lawful remedial discretion: [Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc — Relief removes invalidated predicates; Remand permits lawful relief without requiring a personal zone](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Madsen_v_Womens_Health_Center_Inc_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. O'Connor's Part IV-B, joined by all eight other Justices: nine at the invalidated-predicate and no-evasion level. This agreement does not settle the permissible content of a new decree. O'Connor's Part IV-C, joined by Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg: eight. Stone-Zsela dissents from the refusal to require his bounded post-refusal replacement.

- Coverage boundary retained in applying the speech-injunction rule: [Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc — An injunction binds responsible parties and actual concert, not a viewpoint](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Madsen_v_Womens_Health_Center_Inc_merits_1994-06-30.md#public-projection), 1994-06-30. O'Connor's Part II-A(1), joined by all eight other Justices: nine. The three dissenters on operational neutrality join only this distinct coverage principle.

**Present operation effective:** Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc, 1994-06-30.

**Exceptions and limits:** The particular distances and hours adjudicated are applications, not universal safe harbors. The decision does not require a personal buffer zone, decide the reserved independent conduct-language claims, or transplant Casey’s abortion-regulation framework into the advocacy inquiry. Coverage remains confined to responsible parties and persons with notice acting in actual concert or participation; viewpoint, association or independent advocacy alone does not establish concert.

**Holdings navigation:** [An injunction binds responsible parties and actual concert, not a viewpoint](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#an-injunction-binds-responsible-parties-and-actual-concert-not-a-viewpoint); [Relief removes invalidated predicates](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#relief-removes-invalidated-predicates); [Remand permits lawful relief without requiring a personal zone](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#remand-permits-lawful-relief-without-requiring-a-personal-zone); [The decree as confined is not categorically content based](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#the-decree-as-confined-is-not-categorically-content-based); [A neutral speech injunction must burden no more speech than necessary](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#a-neutral-speech-injunction-must-burden-no-more-speech-than-necessary).

## Standards and Tests selection: Constitutional structure

## Constitutional structure

### Germane military judicial assignments and fixed tenure

**Governing question:** Whether commissioned military judges need another appointment or fixed terms in the statutory system reviewed in Weiss.

**Current rule:** The Appointments Clause applies, but presidential appointment and Senate confirmation to a military office suffice for assignment of germane trial and appellate judicial duties; assignment does not confer a new unrelated office. Due process does not require fixed terms merely because those assignments lack specified duration: Middendorf’s military-specific inquiry considers history and actual statutory protections against command influence. The facial holding does not require proof of actual capitulation to challenge a constitutionally intolerable structure, or preclude supported retaliation, improper reassignment or command-influence claims.

**Authority by component:**

- Existing commission and germane-duty appointment rule: [Weiss v. United States — Commissioned officers assigned germane military judicial duties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Weiss_v_United_States_merits_1994-01-19.md#public-projection), 1994-01-19. Part A of Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; nine Justices support the confined existing-commission and germane-duty proposition.
- Contextual due-process tenure holding: [Weiss v. United States — Fixed tenure and impartial military adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/terms/OT1993/records/Weiss_v_United_States_merits_1994-01-19.md#public-projection), 1994-01-19. Part B of Kennedy's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Ginsburg; seven Justices. Scalia and Thomas concur in the judgment on the distinct historical-practice ground and join the historical description, but do not join Part B's contextual sufficiency analysis.

**Present operation effective:** Weiss v. United States, 1994-01-19.

**Open question:** Civilian appellate appointments, unrelated or principal-office assignments and every possible structural-bias claim remain undecided.

**Holdings navigation:** [Commissioned officers assigned germane military judicial duties](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#commissioned-officers-assigned-germane-military-judicial-duties); [Fixed tenure and impartial military adjudication](https://github.com/Alex-Oss222/scotus-career/blob/main/state/HOLDINGS.md#fixed-tenure-and-impartial-military-adjudication).
